(\varrho^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\,\mu_{1}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\ldots\nu_{m}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}=(\vartheta^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\,\nu_{1}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\ldots\nu_{q}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}
.
By orthogonality,
this means that
\varrho=\vartheta

and
m=q
, and moreover

(\mu_{i}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}=(\nu_{i}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}

for all
1\leq i\leq m
.
By IH, for each
1\leq i\leq m
,
there exist multisteps such that

\hat{\mu}_{i}\uparrow\hat{\nu}_{i}
,
where
\hat{\mu}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\nu}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Taking

\hat{\mu}=\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\varrho\,\hat{\mu}_{1}\ldots\hat{\mu}_{m}

and

\hat{\nu}=\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\varrho\,\hat{\nu}_{1}\ldots\hat{\nu}_{m}

it is then easy to check that

\hat{\mu}\uparrow\hat{\nu}
,
and moreover
\hat{\mu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\nu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}
.

Thus for arbitrary, coinitial multisteps
\mu
and
\nu
, it suffices to show that we can always find corresponding
compatible
“almost”
\eta
-expanded and
\beta
-normal forms, as mentioned above.

Proposition 39

(Existence and uniqueness of projection)
.

Let
\mu,\nu
be such that
\mu^{\mathsf{src}}=_{\beta\eta}\nu^{\mathsf{src}}
.
Then:

1.

Existence.

There exist multisteps
\dot{\mu},\dot{\nu},\dot{\xi}

such that
\dot{\mu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\dot{\nu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\dot{\mu}/\!\!/\!\!/\dot{\nu}\Rightarrow\dot{\xi}
.

2.

Compatibility.

Furthermore,
\dot{\mu}
and
\dot{\nu}
can
be chosen in such a way that
\dot{\mu}\uparrow\dot{\nu}
.

3.

Uniqueness.

If

(\dot{\mu}^{\prime})^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and

(\dot{\nu}^{\prime})^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}

and

\dot{\mu}^{\prime}/\!\!/\!\!/\dot{\nu}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\dot{\xi}^{\prime}

then
(\dot{\xi}^{\prime})^{\mathcal{F}}=\xi^{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof 6.3
.

Note that
\mu^{\blacktriangleleft}=\nu^{\blacktriangleleft}
,
so by Lemma
38

there exist multisteps
\hat{\mu},\hat{\nu}

such that
\hat{\mu}\uparrow\hat{\nu}

and moreover
\hat{\mu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\nu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Since for any
\mu\uparrow\nu
, there exists a unique
\xi

such that
\mu/\!\!/\!\!/\nu\Rightarrow\xi
, we deduce that there exists a multistep
\hat{\xi}

such that
\hat{\mu}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}
.
This proves items 1. and 2.
For item 3., first a
claim.
Let
\mu/\!\!/\!\!/\nu\Rightarrow\xi
.
Then there exist multisteps

\hat{\mu},\hat{\nu},\hat{\xi}

such that:

1.

\mu\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\hat{\mu}

and

\nu\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\hat{\nu}

and

\xi\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\hat{\xi}

2.

\hat{\mu}\uparrow\hat{\nu}

3.

\hat{\mu}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}

Proof of the claim.
First, if
\mu\uparrow\nu
holds, we are done.
Otherwise, by strong normalization of flattening (Proposition
28
)
it suffices to show that
if
\mu/\!\!/\!\!/\nu\Rightarrow\xi

and
\mu\uparrow\nu
does not hold,
then there exist steps

\mu\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}\hat{\mu}

and

\nu\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}\hat{\nu}

and

\xi\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}\hat{\xi}

such that
\hat{\mu}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}
.
This can be proved by induction on the derivation of

\mu/\!\!/\!\!/\nu\Rightarrow\xi
.
End of proof of claim.

Now suppose that

\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{\nu}^{\prime},\hat{\xi}^{\prime}
are such that

(\hat{\mu}^{\prime})^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and

(\hat{\nu}^{\prime})^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}

and

\hat{\mu}^{\prime}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}^{\prime}
.
By the claim this implies that
there exist multisteps
\hat{\mu}^{\prime\prime},\hat{\nu}^{\prime\prime},\hat{\xi}^{\prime\prime}

such that

\hat{\mu}^{\prime}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\hat{\mu}^{\prime\prime}

and

\hat{\nu}^{\prime}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\hat{\nu}^{\prime\prime}

and

\hat{\xi}^{\prime}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\hat{\xi}^{\prime\prime}
,
and moreover

\hat{\mu}^{\prime\prime}\uparrow\hat{\nu}^{\prime\prime}

and
\hat{\mu}^{\prime\prime}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}^{\prime\prime}
.
Note that

\hat{\mu}^{\prime\mathcal{F}}=\hat{\mu}^{\prime\prime\mathcal{F}}

given that
\hat{\mu}^{\prime}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\hat{\mu}^{\prime\prime}
.
Similarly,

\hat{\nu}^{\prime\mathcal{F}}=\hat{\nu}^{\prime\prime\mathcal{F}}

and

\hat{\xi}^{\prime\mathcal{F}}=\hat{\xi}^{\prime\prime\mathcal{F}}
.
Hence by Lemma
37

we may conclude that

\hat{\xi}^{\mathcal{F}}=\hat{\xi}^{\prime\mathcal{F}}
,
as required.

We can now define projection on arbitrary coinitial rewrites as follows.

{defi}

[Projection operator for multisteps]

Let
\mu,\nu
be such that
\mu^{\mathsf{src}}=_{\beta\eta}\nu^{\mathsf{src}}
.
We write
\mu/\nu

for the unique multistep of the form
\dot{\xi}^{\mathcal{F}}

such that there exist
\dot{\mu},\dot{\nu}

such that
\dot{\mu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\dot{\nu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\dot{\mu}/\!\!/\!\!/\dot{\nu}\Rightarrow\dot{\xi}
,
as guaranteed by Proposition
39
.
The proof is constructive (this relies on the HRS being orthogonal),
thus providing an effective method to compute
\mu/\nu
.

Proposition 40

(Properties of projection for multisteps)
.

1.

\mu/\nu=(\mu/\nu)^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}/\nu^{\mathcal{F}}

2.

Projection commutes with abstraction and application,
that is,

(\lambda x.\mu)/(\lambda x.\nu)=(\lambda x.(\mu/\nu))^{\mathcal{F}}

and

(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})/(\nu_{1}\,\nu_{2})=((\mu_{1}/\nu_{1})\,(\mu_{2}/\nu_{2}))^{\mathcal{F}}
,
provided that

\mu_{1}/\nu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}/\nu_{2}

are defined.

3.

The set of multisteps with the projection operator form
a residual system
[
Ter03
, Def. 8.7.2]
:

3.1

(\mu/\nu)/(\xi/\nu)=(\mu/\xi)/(\nu/\xi)
,
known as the
Cube Lemma
.

3.2

\mu/\mu=(\mu^{\mathsf{tgt}})^{\mathcal{F}}

and, as particular cases:

\underline{s}/\underline{s}=\underline{s}^{\mathcal{F}}
,

x/x=x
,

\mathbf{c}/\mathbf{c}=\mathbf{c}
, and

\varrho/\varrho=(\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}})^{\mathcal{F}}
.

3.3

(\mu^{\mathsf{src}})^{\mathcal{F}}/\mu=(\mu^{\mathsf{tgt}})^{\mathcal{F}}

and, as a particular case,

(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}})^{\mathcal{F}}/\varrho=(\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}})^{\mathcal{F}}
.

3.4

\mu/(\mu^{\mathsf{src}})^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and, as a particular case,

\varrho/(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}})^{\mathcal{F}}=\varrho
.

Proof 6.4
.

We prove each item:

1.

\mu/\nu=(\mu/\nu)^{\mathcal{F}}
:
By definition
there exist
\hat{\mu},\hat{\nu},\hat{\xi}

such that
\hat{\mu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\nu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\mu}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}

where
\mu/\nu=\hat{\xi}^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then

\mu/\nu=\hat{\xi}^{\mathcal{F}}=(\hat{\xi}^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu/\nu)^{\mathcal{F}}
.

2.

\mu/\nu=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}/\nu^{\mathcal{F}}
:
By definition of
\mu/\nu

there exist
\hat{\mu},\hat{\nu},\hat{\xi}

such that
\hat{\mu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\nu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\mu}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}

where
\mu/\nu=\hat{\xi}^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then since
\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\nu^{\mathcal{F}}=(\nu^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}
,
the triple
\hat{\mu},\hat{\nu},\hat{\xi}

also fulfills the conditions for the definition of

\mu^{\mathcal{F}}/\nu^{\mathcal{F}}
.
But Proposition
39
ensures uniqueness,
so

\mu/\nu=\hat{\xi}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}/\nu^{\mathcal{F}}
.

3.

\mu/\mu=\mu^{\blacktriangleright}
:
It suffices to note that
\mu/\!\!/\!\!/\mu\Rightarrow\mu^{\mathsf{tgt}}

holds, as can be checked by induction on
\mu
.

4.

\mu/\mu^{\blacktriangleleft}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}
:
It suffices to note that

\mu/\!\!/\!\!/\mu^{\mathsf{src}}\Rightarrow\mu
,
as can be checked by induction on
\mu
.

5.

\mu^{\blacktriangleleft}/\mu=\mu^{\blacktriangleright}
:
It suffices to note that

\mu^{\mathsf{src}}/\!\!/\!\!/\mu\Rightarrow\mu^{\mathsf{tgt}}
,
as can be checked by induction on
\mu
.

6.

(\lambda x.\mu)/(\lambda x.\nu)=(\lambda x.(\mu/\nu))^{\mathcal{F}}
:
Observe that the left-hand side of the equation
is defined if and only if the right-hand
side is defined, given that

(\lambda x.\mu)^{\mathsf{src}}=_{\beta\eta}(\lambda x.\nu)^{\mathsf{src}}

if and only if
\mu^{\mathsf{src}}=_{\beta\eta}\nu^{\mathsf{src}}
,
which is easy to check.

By definition of
\mu/\nu

there exist
\hat{\mu},\hat{\nu},\hat{\xi}

such that
\hat{\mu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\nu}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\mu}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}

where
\mu/\nu=\hat{\xi}^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then note, by confluence of flattening (Proposition
29
),
that

(\lambda x.\hat{\mu})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x.\hat{\mu}^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x.\mu^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x.\mu)^{\mathcal{F}}

and, similarly,

(\lambda x.\hat{\nu})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x.\nu)^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Moreover, by the
ProjAbs
rule,

\lambda x.\hat{\mu}/\!\!/\!\!/\lambda x.\hat{\nu}\Rightarrow\lambda x.\hat{\xi}
.
By uniqueness of projection (Proposition
39
)
this means that

(\lambda x.\mu)/(\lambda x.\nu)=(\lambda x.\hat{\xi})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x.\hat{\xi}^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x.(\mu/\nu))^{\mathcal{F}}
.

7.

(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})/(\nu_{1}\,\nu_{2})=((\mu_{1}/\nu_{1})\,(\mu_{2}/\nu_{2}))^{\mathcal{F}}
:
Observe that, if the right-hand side of the equation is defined,
then the left-hand side is also defined, given that
if
\mu_{1}^{\mathsf{src}}=_{\beta\eta}\nu_{1}^{\mathsf{src}}

and
\mu_{2}^{\mathsf{src}}=_{\beta\eta}\nu_{2}^{\mathsf{src}}

then

(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathsf{src}}=_{\beta\eta}(\nu_{1}\,\nu_{2})^{\mathsf{src}}
.

Note that, by hypothesis, the right-hand side of the equation is defined.
By definition of
\mu_{1}/\nu_{1}

there exist
\hat{\mu}_{1},\hat{\nu}_{1},\hat{\xi}_{1}

such that
\hat{\mu}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\nu}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\mu}_{1}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}_{1}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}_{1}

where
\mu_{1}/\nu_{1}=\hat{\xi}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Similarly,
by definition of
\mu_{2}/\nu_{2}

there exist
\hat{\mu}_{2},\hat{\nu}_{2},\hat{\xi}_{2}

such that
\hat{\mu}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\nu}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\hat{\mu}_{2}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}_{2}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}_{2}

where
\mu_{2}/\nu_{2}=\hat{\xi}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}
.

Note, by confluence of flattening (Proposition
29
),

(\hat{\mu}_{1}\,\hat{\mu}_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\hat{\mu}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\,\hat{\mu}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\,\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}
and,
similarly,

(\hat{\nu}_{1}\,\hat{\nu}_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\nu_{1}\,\nu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Moreover, by the
ProjApp
rule,

\hat{\mu}_{1}\,\hat{\mu}_{2}/\!\!/\!\!/\hat{\nu}_{1}\,\hat{\nu}_{2}\Rightarrow\hat{\xi}_{1}\,\hat{\xi}_{2}
.
By uniqueness of projection Proposition
39

this means that

\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2}/\nu_{1}\,\nu_{2}=(\hat{\xi}_{1}\,\hat{\xi}_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\hat{\xi}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\,\hat{\xi}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}=((\mu_{1}/\nu_{1})\,(\mu_{2}/\nu_{2}))^{\mathcal{F}}
.

{exa}

Let
\vartheta:\lambda x.\mathbf{f}\,x\to\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,x
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{lll}(\lambda x.(\lambda x.\mathbf{f}\,x)\,x)/(\lambda x.\vartheta\,x)&=&(\lambda x.((\lambda x.\mathbf{f}\,x)\,x)/(\vartheta\,x))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
&=&(\lambda x.(((\lambda x.\mathbf{f}\,x)/\vartheta)(x/x))^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}\\
&=&(\lambda x.((\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,x)\,x)^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}\\
&=&(\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,x)^{\mathcal{F}}\\
&=&\mathbf{g}\end{array}

6.2.
Projection for Flat Rewrites.

The projection operator from Definition
6.1

is extended to operate on flat rewrites.
One may try to define

\rho/\sigma

using equations such as

(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})/\sigma=(\rho_{1}/\sigma)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/(\sigma/\rho_{1}))
.
However, it is not
a priori
clear that this recursive definition
is well-founded
5
5

5

Another way to prove
well-foundedness is by interpretation,
as done in
[
Ter03
, Example 6.5.43]
.
.
This is why the following definition proceeds in three stages:

{defi}

[Projection operator for flat rewrites]

We define:

1.

projection of a flat multistep over a coinitial flat rewrite
(
\mu\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho
),
by induction on
\rho
;

2.

projection of a flat rewrite over a coinitial flat multistep
(
\rho\mathrel{/^{2}}\mu
),
by induction on
\rho
;
and

3.

projection of a flat rewrite over a coinitial flat rewrite
(
\rho\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma
)
by induction on
\sigma
,
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\hspace{1cm}}rcl}\mu\mathrel{/^{1}}\nu&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&\mu/\nu\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\mu\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&(\mu\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{1})\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{2}\\
\nu\mathrel{/^{2}}\mu&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&\nu/\mu\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})\mathrel{/^{2}}\mu&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&(\rho_{1}\mathrel{/^{2}}\mu)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}\mathrel{/^{2}}(\mu\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{1}))\\
\rho\mathrel{/^{3}}\mu&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&\rho\mathrel{/^{2}}\mu\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\rho\mathrel{/^{3}}(\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2})&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&(\rho\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma_{1})\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma_{2}\end{array}

Note that
\mathrel{/^{3}}
generalizes
\mathrel{/^{2}}
and
\mathrel{/^{1}}

in the sense that
\mu\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho=\mu\mathrel{/^{3}}\rho

and
\rho\mathrel{/^{2}}\mu=\rho\mathrel{/^{3}}\mu
.
With these definitions, the key equation

(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma=(\rho_{1}\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}\mathrel{/^{3}}(\sigma\mathrel{/^{3}}\rho_{1}))

can be shown to hold.
From this point on, we overload
\rho/\sigma

to stand for either of these projection operators.
The key equation ensures that this abuse of notation is harmless.

Next we address some important properties of projection
for flat rewrites.
First, projection of a rewrite over a sequence, and of a sequence
over a rewrite, obey the expected equations

\rho/(\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2})=(\rho/\sigma_{1})/\sigma_{2}

and

(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})/\sigma=(\rho_{1}/\sigma)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/(\sigma/\rho_{1}))
.
The former follows from Definition
6.2
and the latter is
proved by induction on
|\rho_{1}|+|\rho_{2}|+|\sigma|
for

|\cdot|
an appropriate notion of size of rewrites. Second, flat
permutation equivalence is a congruence with respect to projection:
if
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma

and
\tau
is an arbitrary flat rewrite
coinitial to
\rho
, then both
(1)
\tau/\rho=\tau/\sigma
;
and (2)
\rho/\tau\mathrel{\sim}\sigma/\tau
(Proposition
49
).
The proof of both items is by induction on
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma
; the more challenging case is when
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma
follows from the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
rule:

•

For (1), we prove ():
If
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

and
\rho
is an arbitrary flat rewrite coinitial to
\mu_{1}
,
then
\rho/\mu_{1}=(\rho/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3}
.

•

For (2), we prove (Proposition
46
):
If
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

and
\nu
is an arbitrary multistep coinitial to
\mu_{1}
,
then

(\mu_{1}/\nu)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}(\mu_{2}/\nu)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\nu/\mu_{2}))
.

Lemma 41
.

If
(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})
and
\sigma
are coinitial flat
rewrites, then:

(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma=(\rho_{1}\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}\mathrel{/^{3}}(\sigma\mathrel{/^{3}}\rho_{1}))

Proof 6.5
.

The proof is by induction on
|\rho_{1}|+|\rho_{2}|+|\sigma|
.

Lemma 42

(Basic properties of rewrite projection)
.

Let
\rho
stand for a flat rewrite.
Then both

\rho^{\blacktriangleleft}/\rho=\rho^{\blacktriangleright}
and

\rho/\rho^{\blacktriangleleft}=\rho
hold.

Proof 6.6
.

Both items are proved by induction on
\rho
and make use of Proposition
40
. We present the proof of the second one as a sample.
If
\rho=\mu
is a multistep,
then
\mu/\mu^{\blacktriangleleft}=\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu

by Proposition
40
, using the fact that

\rho
is flat by hypothesis.
If
\rho=\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}
then:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}&&(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})/(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})^{\blacktriangleleft}\\
&=&(\rho_{1}/(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})^{\blacktriangleleft})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/((\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})^{\blacktriangleleft}/\rho_{1}))&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:projrr_sequence}}\\
&=&(\rho_{1}/(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})^{\blacktriangleleft})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}^{\blacktriangleleft}/\rho_{1}))\\
&=&(\rho_{1}/(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})^{\blacktriangleleft})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/\rho_{1}^{\blacktriangleright})&\text{by item~1. of this lemma}\\
&=&(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1}^{\blacktriangleleft})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/\rho_{2}^{\blacktriangleleft})&\text{as $\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}^{\mathsf{src}}=\rho_{1}^{\mathsf{src}}$
and $\rho_{1}^{\mathsf{tgt}}=\rho_{2}^{\mathsf{src}}$}\\
&=&\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}&\text{by IH }\end{array}

First consider the relation
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}}
, which is defined like
\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}

but without the
\mathcal{F}
-EtaM
rule.

Lemma 43

(Canonical
\mapsto^{\circ},\overline{\eta}
-normal splitting)
.

If
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

then there exist
\mu^{\prime}_{1},\mu^{\prime}_{2},\mu^{\prime}_{3}

such that
\mu^{\prime}_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}

where

\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\prime}_{1})^{\mathcal{F}}

and

\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\prime}_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}

and

\mu_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\prime}_{3})^{\mathcal{F}}
,
and moreover
\mu^{\prime}
is in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form.

Proof 6.7
.

Suppose
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}
.
Then
we have that

\mu^{\prime\prime}_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime\prime}_{3}

where

\mu^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\mu_{2}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\mu^{\prime\prime}_{2}

and
\mu_{3}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\mu^{\prime\prime}_{3}
.
This fact may be proved by induction on the length of a reduction to normal form
\mu_{1}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}
. One then proceeds by induction on the shape of
\mu_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
it suffices to show that there exist
\mu^{\prime}_{1},\mu^{\prime}_{2},\mu^{\prime}_{3}

such that

\mu^{\prime}_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}

where
(\mu^{\prime}_{1})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\mathcal{F}}

and
(\mu^{\prime}_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\prime\prime}_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}

and
(\mu^{\prime}_{3})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\prime\prime}_{3})^{\mathcal{F}}
,
and moreover
\mu^{\prime}_{1}
is in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form. This is done by direct inspection considering each of the cases:
\mu^{\prime\prime}_{1}
headed by a variable,
\mu^{\prime\prime}_{1}
headed by a constant, and
\mu^{\prime\prime}_{1}
headed by a rule symbol.

{defi}

[
\eta
-expanded source]
If
\mu
is a multistep in
\overline{\eta}
-normal form,
we write
\mu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}
for the source of
\mu

in which the sources of rule symbols are also
\eta
-expanded.
More precisely:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}x^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&x\\
\mathbf{c}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&\mathbf{c}\\
\varrho^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&s^{\prime}&\text{
if $\varrho:s\rightarrowtriangle t:A\in\mathcal{R}$
and $s^{\prime}$ is the $\overline{\eta}$-normal form of $s$
}\\
(\lambda x.\mu)^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&\lambda x.s^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\\
(\mu\,\nu)^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&\mu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\,\nu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\end{array}

Note that
\mu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\mu^{\mathsf{src}}

so, in particular,

(\mu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\mathsf{src}})^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu^{\blacktriangleleft}
.

Lemma 44

(Flattening of
\eta
-expanded multisteps)
.

Let
\mu,\nu

be multisteps in
\overline{\eta}
-normal form
such that

\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=\nu^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then
\mu^{\circ}=\nu^{\circ}
.

Proof 6.8
.

This essentially follows from the fact that
\mathcal{F}
-EtaM
-redexes
may be postponed after
\mathcal{F}
-BetaM
-redexes
(a standard result, regarding multisteps
as terms of the simply-typed
\lambda
-calculus) and that
flattening preserves

\overline{\eta}
-normal forms.

Lemma 45

(Constructor/rule matching)
.

Let
\mu=\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{m}

and also
let
\nu=\lambda y_{1}\ldots y_{p}.\varrho\,\nu_{1}\ldots\nu_{q}
.
Let
\varrho:l_{0}\rightarrowtriangle r_{0}:A\in\mathcal{R}

and let
l,r
be the
\mathrel{\,\to_{\overline{\eta}}\,}
-normal forms of
l_{0},r_{0}
respectively.
Suppose that
\mu^{\mathsf{src}}=_{\beta\eta}\nu^{\mathsf{src}}

and, moreover, that
\mu
and
\nu
are in

\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form. Then there exist multisteps

\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{q}
such that:

\mu=(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\underline{l}\,\xi_{1}\ldots\xi_{q})^{\circ}

and

(\xi_{i}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}=(\nu_{i}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}

for all
1\leq i\leq q
.

Furthermore, the multisteps
\xi_{i}

can be chosen in such a way that they are in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form,
and the number of applications in each
multistep
\xi_{i}
, for all
1\leq i\leq k
,
is strictly less than the number of applications in
\mu
.

Proof 6.9
.

Note that the condition
\mu^{\mathsf{src}}=_{\beta\eta}\nu^{\mathsf{src}}

implies that
(\mu^{\mathsf{src}})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\nu^{\mathsf{src}})^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then also
(\mu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\nu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\mathcal{F}}
.
By Lemma
44
this implies that:

(\mu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}=(\nu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}

(6)

Suppose

l=\lambda z_{1}.\ldots\lambda z_{k}.\mathbf{d}\,r_{1}\ldots r_{k^{\prime}}
.
From (
6
) we have:

(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\ldots\mu_{m}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}=(\lambda y_{1}\ldots y_{p}.l\,\nu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}_{1}\ldots\nu_{q}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}

Since

\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\ldots\mu_{m}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}
and

l\,\nu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}_{1}\ldots\nu_{q}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}
have base
types, then
n=p

and we may assume that
x_{i}=y_{i}
, for all
1\leq i\leq p
.
Moreover,

(\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\ldots\mu_{m}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}=(l\,\nu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}_{1}\ldots\nu_{q}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}

Therefore
\mathbf{d}=\mathbf{c}
,
q=k
and
m=k^{\prime}
, and:

(\mu_{i}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}=(r_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\{}}}z_{1}\backslash\nu^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\}}}}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\{}}}z_{k}\backslash\nu_{k}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\}}}})^{\circ}

for all
1\leq i\leq m
.
Note that each
r_{i}
is part of the pattern of
the rewrite rule
l
. By orthogonality of
\mathcal{R}
,
there exist
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}
such that:

\mu_{i}=(r_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\{}}}z_{1}\backslash\xi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\}}}}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\{}}}z_{k}\backslash\xi_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\}}}})^{\circ}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all $1\leq i\leq m$}

and

(\xi_{i}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}=(\nu_{i}^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}})^{\circ}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all $1\leq i\leq k$}

Therefore:

\mu=\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{m}=(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.l\,\xi_{1}\ldots\xi_{k})^{\circ}

Furthermore, we claim that each
\xi_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq k
,
can be chosen in such a way that it is in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form
and with strictly less applications than
\mu
.
First, note that if the
\xi_{i}
are not in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}}
-normal form,
then we may take
\hat{\xi}_{i}:=\xi_{i}^{\circ}

instead, and we still have that

\mu=(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.l\,\xi_{1}\ldots\xi_{k})^{\circ}=(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.l\,\hat{\xi}_{1}\ldots\hat{\xi}_{k})^{\circ}

by confluence of flattening (Proposition
29
).
So let us assume that the
\xi_{i}
are in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}}
-normal form.
Furthermore, suppose that

\xi_{i}
is of arity
N
,
i.e. its type is of the form

A_{1}\to\ldots\to A_{N}\to\alpha

with
\alpha
a base type,
and suppose that

\xi_{i}=\lambda w_{1}\ldots w_{M}.\xi^{\prime}_{i}

where
\xi^{\prime}_{i}
is not a
\lambda
-abstraction.
Note that
M\leq N
.
Take

\hat{\xi}_{i}:=\lambda w_{1}\ldots w_{M}w_{M+1}\ldots w_{N}.\xi^{\prime}_{i}\,w_{M+1}\ldots w_{N}
.
Note that each
\hat{\xi}_{i}
is in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}}
-normal form.

To conclude, we claim that

\mu=(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\underline{l}\,\hat{\xi}_{1}\ldots\hat{\xi}_{k})^{\circ}

and that each
\hat{\xi}_{i}

has strictly less applications than
\mu
.
To see this, note that
each variable
z_{1},\ldots,z_{k}
.
occurs free exactly once in the body of
l
,
applied to different bound variables.
More precisely, for a fixed index
1\leq i_{0}\leq k

there is exactly one
1\leq j_{0}\leq m

such that
z_{i_{0}}
occurs free in
r_{j}
.
Then the multistep that results from substituting in
r_{j}

each
z_{i}
by
\hat{\xi}_{i}
other than for
i=i_{0}

contains exactly one occurrence of
z_{i_{0}}

applied to
N
variables, where
N
is the arity of
\hat{\xi}_{i_{0}}
,
that is, it is of the form:

r_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\{}}}z_{1}\backslash\xi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\}}}}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\{}}}z_{(i_{0}-1)}\backslash\xi_{(i_{0}-1)}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\{}}}z_{(i_{0}+1)}\backslash\xi_{(i_{0}+1)}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\}}}}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\{}}}z_{k}\backslash\xi_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\bm{\}}}}=\mathtt{C}\langle z_{i_{0}}\,w_{1}\ldots w_{N}\rangle

where
w_{1},\ldots,w_{N}
are bound by
\mathtt{C}

and the hole of
\mathtt{C}
is not applied.
As a consequence, we have that:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mu&=&\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\mathbf{c}\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{i_{0}-1}\,\mathtt{C}\langle(\hat{\xi}_{i_{0}}\,w_{1}\ldots\,w_{N})^{\circ}\rangle\,\mu_{i_{0}+1}\ldots\mu_{m}\\
&=&\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\mathbf{c}\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{i_{0}-1}\,\mathtt{C}\langle((\lambda w_{1}\ldots w_{N}.\xi_{i_{0}}\,w_{M+1}\ldots w_{N})\,w_{1}\ldots\,w_{N})^{\circ}\rangle\,\mu_{i_{0}+1}\ldots\mu_{m}\\
&=&\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\mathbf{c}\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{i_{0}-1}\,\mathtt{C}\langle\xi_{i_{0}}\,w_{M+1}\ldots w_{N}\rangle\,\mu_{i_{0}+1}\ldots\mu_{m}\end{array}

Hence, since
\mu
is in
\overline{\eta}
-normal form,
and
\xi_{i_{0}}\,w_{M+1}\ldots w_{N}

is a subterm of
\mu
which is not applied,
we know that
\xi_{i_{0}}\,w_{M+1}\ldots w_{N}

is in
\overline{\eta}
-normal form.
Hence

\hat{\xi}_{i_{0}}=\lambda w_{1}\ldots w_{n}.\xi_{i_{0}}\,w_{M+1}\ldots w_{N}

is also in
\overline{\eta}
-normal form.

Finally,
note that

(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\underline{l}\,\hat{\xi}_{1}\ldots\hat{\xi}_{k})^{\circ}

is in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form
and that
we have that

\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\underline{l}\,\xi_{1}\ldots\xi_{k})^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\underline{l}\,\hat{\xi}_{1}\ldots\hat{\xi}_{k})^{\mathcal{F}}

so by Lemma
44

we have that

\mu^{\circ}=(\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{n}.\underline{l}\,\hat{\xi}_{1}\ldots\hat{\xi}_{k})^{\circ}
.
Moreover, for each
1\leq i_{0}\leq k

the multistep

\hat{\xi}_{i_{0}}=\lambda x_{1}\ldots x_{N}.\xi_{i_{0}}\,x_{M+1}\ldots x_{N}

has the same number of applications as

\xi_{i_{0}}\,x_{M+1}\ldots x_{M}
,
which is a subterm of
\mu
,
and has strictly less applications than
\mu
.

Proposition 46

(Generalized
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
rule)
.

If
\mu\Leftrightarrow\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}

then
\mu^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}
.

Note that this generalizes the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
rule,
which requires
\mu
to be in
\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}
-normal form.

Proof 6.10
.

Suppose
\mu\Leftrightarrow\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}
.
Then
we have that

\mu^{\mathcal{F}}\Leftrightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{2}

such that
\mu_{1}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\mu^{\prime}_{1}

and
\mu_{2}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\mu^{\prime}_{2}
. This fact may be proved by induction on the length of a reduction
to normal form
\mu\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\mu^{\mathcal{F}}
.
By the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
rule,

\mu^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{\sim}(\mu^{\prime}_{1})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Moreover, by strong normalization (Proposition
28
)
and confluence (Proposition
29
) of flattening,

\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\prime}_{1})^{\mathcal{F}}

and

\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\prime}_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}
,
which means that

\mu^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}
.

{defi}

[Splitting, up to flattening]
We write
\mu_{1}\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

if there exist multisteps
\mu^{\prime}_{1},\mu^{\prime}_{2},\mu^{\prime}_{3}

such that
\mu^{\prime}_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}
,
and moreover
(\mu^{\prime}_{1})^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{1}

and
(\mu^{\prime}_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{2}

and
(\mu^{\prime}_{3})^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{3}
.

Lemma 47

(Projection of a splitting over a multistep)
.

If
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

and
\nu
is an arbitrary multistep coinitial to
\mu_{1}
,
then

(\mu_{1}/\nu)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}(\mu_{2}/\nu)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\nu/\mu_{2}))
.

Proof 6.11
.

Suppose that

\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}
.
Recall from Proposition
40

that
\mu_{1}/\nu
does not depend on the representative
of
\mu_{1}
(up to flattening),
and similarly for
\mu_{2}/\nu
and
\mu_{3}/(\nu/\mu_{2})
.
Hence
by Lemma
43
we may assume without loss of generality
that
\mu_{1}
is in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form.
We may also assume without loss of generality that
\nu
is in

\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form.
We show that

(\mu_{1}/\nu)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}(\mu_{2}/\nu)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\nu/\mu_{2}))

holds,
by induction on the number of applications in
\mu_{1}

and by case analysis on the head of
\mu_{1}
, using the fact that it is
in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form. We omit the cases where
\mu_{1}
headed
by a variable or a constant and present the one where it is headed by a rule
symbol.

Then
\mu_{1}=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,\mu_{11}\ldots\mu_{1n}
.
Note that
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

must be derived by a number of instances of the
SAbs
rule,
followed by
n
instances of
SApp
rule,
followed by an instance
of either
SRuleL
or
SRuleL
at the head.
We consider two subcases:

1.

SRuleL
:
Then

\mu_{2}=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,\mu_{21}\ldots\mu_{2n}

and

\mu_{3}=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,\mu_{31}\ldots\mu_{3n}

where
\mu_{1i}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3i}

for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Since
\mu_{1}
and
\nu
are coinitial,
the head of
\nu
must be a constant or a rule symbol.
We consider two further subcases:

1.1

\nu
headed by a constant.

By Lemma
45

we have that

\nu=(\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\,\nu_{1}\ldots\nu_{n})^{\circ}
,
where
\mu_{1i}
and
\nu_{i}
are coinitial
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
By Proposition
40
we have that:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mu_{1}/\nu&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}})(\mu_{11}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{1n}/\nu_{n}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
\mu_{2}/\nu&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}})(\mu_{21}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{2n}/\nu_{n}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
\mu_{3}/(\nu/\mu_{2})&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}/(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}/\varrho))(\mu_{31}/(\nu_{1}/\mu_{21}))\ldots(\mu_{3n}/(\nu_{n}/\mu_{2n})))^{\mathcal{F}}\end{array}

By IH we know that

(\mu_{1i}/\nu_{i})\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}(\mu_{2i}/\nu_{i})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3i}/(\nu_{i}/\mu_{2i}))
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Moreover,
note that

\varrho/\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft}=\varrho

and

\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}/(\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft}/\varrho)=\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}/\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}=\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}

by Proposition
40
,
and that
\varrho\Leftrightarrow\varrho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}
.
Hence:

\begin{array}[]{rrl}\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,(\mu_{11}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{1n}/\nu_{n})&\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}&\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,(\mu_{21}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{2n}/\nu_{n})\\
&\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}&\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,(\mu_{31}/(\nu_{1}/\mu_{21}))\ldots(\mu_{3n}/(\nu_{n}/\mu_{2n}))\end{array}

This in turn implies that

\mu_{1}/\nu\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}\mu_{2}/\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}/(\nu/\mu_{1})
.

1.2

\nu
headed by a rule symbol.

Then by orthogonality

\nu=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,\nu_{1}\ldots\nu_{n}
.
By Proposition
40
we have that:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mu_{1}/\nu&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho/\varrho)(\mu_{11}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{1n}/\nu_{n}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
\mu_{2}/\nu&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho/\varrho)(\mu_{21}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{2n}/\nu_{n}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
\mu_{3}/(\nu/\mu_{2})&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}/(\varrho/\varrho))(\mu_{31}/(\nu_{1}/\mu_{21}))\ldots(\mu_{3n}/(\nu_{n}/\mu_{2n})))^{\mathcal{F}}\end{array}

Then the proof proceeds similarly
as for case
1.1
,
when the head of
\nu
is a constant,
noting that
\varrho/\varrho=\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}

and that

\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}/(\varrho/\varrho)=\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}/\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}=\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}

by Proposition
40
,
and that
\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}\Leftrightarrow\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}
.

2.

SRuleR
:
Then

\mu_{2}=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}\,\mu_{21}\ldots\mu_{2n}

and

\mu_{3}=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,\mu_{31}\ldots\mu_{3n}

where
\mu_{1i}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3i}

for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Since
\mu_{1}
and
\nu
are coinitial,
the head of
\nu
must be a constant or a rule symbol.
We consider two further subcases:

2.1

\nu
headed by a constant.

By Lemma
45

we have that

\nu=(\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\,\nu_{1}\ldots\nu_{n})^{\circ}
,
where
\mu_{1i}
and
\nu_{i}
are coinitial
for all
1\leq i\leq n
. Hence:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mu_{1}/\nu&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}})\,(\mu_{11}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{1n}/\nu_{n}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
\mu_{2}/\nu&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}})\,(\mu_{21}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{2n}/\nu_{n}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
\mu_{3}/(\nu/\mu_{2})&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho/(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}))\,(\mu_{31}/(\nu_{1}/\mu_{21}))\ldots(\mu_{3n}/(\nu_{n}/\mu_{2n})))^{\mathcal{F}}\end{array}

Then the proof proceeds similarly
as for case
1.1
,
noting that
\varrho/\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft}=\varrho
,
that
\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft}/\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft}=\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft}
,
and that

\varrho/(\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft}/\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft})=\varrho/\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft}=\varrho

by Proposition
40
,
and moreover that

\varrho\Leftrightarrow\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho
.

2.2

\nu
headed by a rule symbol.

Then by orthogonality

\nu=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,\nu_{1}\ldots\nu_{n}
.
Hence:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mu_{1}/\nu&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho/\varrho)\,(\mu_{11}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{1n}/\nu_{n}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
\mu_{2}/\nu&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}/\varrho)\,(\mu_{21}/\nu_{1})\ldots(\mu_{2n}/\nu_{n}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
\mu_{3}/(\nu/\mu_{2})&=&(\lambda x.(\varrho/(\varrho/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}))\,(\mu_{31}/(\nu_{1}/\mu_{21}))\ldots(\mu_{3n}/(\nu_{n}/\mu_{2n})))^{\mathcal{F}}\end{array}

Then the proof proceeds similarly
as for case
1.1
,
noting that
\varrho/\varrho=\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}
,
that
\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft}/\varrho=\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}
,
and that

\varrho/(\varrho/\varrho^{\blacktriangleleft})=\varrho/\varrho=\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}

by Proposition
40
,
and moreover that

\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}\Leftrightarrow\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}
.

Lemma 48

(Projection of splittings and rewrites)
.

Suppose
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}
. Let
\rho
be an arbitrary flat rewrite coinitial to
\mu_{1}
and let
\nu
be an arbitrary multistep coinitial to
\mu_{1}
. Then:

1.

(\mu_{1}/\rho)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}(\mu_{2}/\rho)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\rho/\mu_{2}))
.

2.

\nu/\mu_{1}=(\nu/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3}

3.

\rho/\mu_{1}=(\rho/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3}
.

Proof 6.12
.

The proof of the first item is by induction on
\rho
. The key case is when
\rho
is a multistep (the base case), in which we resort to Lemma
47
. For the second item, suppose that
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}
.
Recall from Proposition
40

that
\nu/\mu_{1}
does not depend on the representative
of
\mu_{1}
(up to flattening),
and similarly for
(\mu/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3}
.
Hence
by Lemma
43
we may assume without loss of generality
that
\mu_{1}
is in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form.
We may also assume without loss of generality that
\nu
is in

\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form.
We proceed by induction on the number of applications in
\mu_{1}

and by case analysis on the head of
\mu_{1}
, using the fact that it is
in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form. The case where
\mu_{1}
headed by a variable relies on Proposition
40
. The case where it is headed by a
constant additionally relies on Lemma
45
and the IH. The case where it is headed by a rule symbol, in addition it makes use of orthogonality of the HRS.

For the third item, we proceed by induction on
\rho
.

1.

Multistep,
\rho=\nu
.

Then this is an immediate consequence of
Lemma
48
(2)

2.

Composition,
\rho=\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}
.

Note by Lemma
48
(1) that

(\mu_{1}/\rho_{1})\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}(\mu_{2}/\rho_{1})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\rho_{1}/\mu_{2}))

holds, that is,
there exist multisteps such that

\hat{\mu}_{1}\Leftrightarrow\hat{\mu}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\hat{\mu}_{3}

and such that

\hat{\mu}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{1}/\rho_{1}

and

\hat{\mu}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{2}/\rho_{1}

and

\hat{\mu}_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{3}/(\rho_{1}/\mu_{2})
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})/\mu_{1}&=&(\rho_{1}/\mu_{1})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/(\mu_{1}/\rho_{1}))\\
&=&((\rho_{1}/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/(\mu_{1}/\rho_{1}))&\text{by IH }\\
&=&((\rho_{1}/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/\hat{\mu}_{1})&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&=&((\rho_{1}/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\rho_{2}/\hat{\mu}_{2})/\hat{\mu}_{3})&\text{by IH }\\
&=&((\rho_{1}/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\rho_{2}/(\mu_{2}/\rho_{1}))/(\mu_{3}/(\rho_{1}/\mu_{2})))&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&=&((\rho_{1}/\mu_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/(\mu_{2}/\rho_{1})))/\mu_{3}\\
&=&((\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3}\end{array}

Given the above, From this point on we can safely overload
\rho/\sigma

to stand for

\rho\mathrel{/^{1}}\sigma
(if
\rho
is a multistep),

\rho\mathrel{/^{2}}\sigma
(if
\sigma
is a multistep),
or
\rho\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma
(in the general case).

Proposition 49

(Congruence of
\mathrel{\sim}
with respect to projection)
.

Let
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma
,
and let
\tau
be an arbitrary flat rewrite
coinitial to
\rho
.
Then:

1.

\tau/\rho=\tau/\sigma

2.

\rho/\tau\mathrel{\sim}\sigma/\tau

Proof 6.13
.

Both items are induction on the derivation of
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma
.
The reflexivity, symmetry, and transitivity cases are immediate.
We analyze the cases in which an axiom is applied at the root,
as well as congruence closure below composition contexts.

1.

First item. The interesting case is when
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma
follows from
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
.
Let
\mu_{1}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}

be derived from
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\tau/\mu_{1}&=&(\tau/\mu_{2})/\mu_{3}&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:projection_of_splittings_and_rewrites}(3)}\\
&=&(\tau/\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}})/\mu_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\end{array}

2.

Second item. The case where
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma
follows from
\mathrel{\sim}
-Assoc
follows from Lemma
41
and
\mathrel{\sim}
-Assoc
itself. Similarly, the case where
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma
follows from congruence of composition (left or right) follows from Lemma
41
, the IH and
item 1. of this proposition. The interesting case, once again, is when
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma
follows from

\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
.
Let
\mu_{1}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}

be derived from
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}
.
Then by Lemma
48
(1) we have that

(\mu_{1}/\tau)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\Leftrightarrow}(\mu_{2}/\tau)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\tau/\mu_{2}))
,
that is, there exist multisteps such that

\hat{\mu}_{1}\Leftrightarrow\hat{\mu}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\hat{\mu}_{3}

and such that

\hat{\mu}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{1}/\tau

and

\hat{\mu}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{2}/\tau

and

\hat{\mu}_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{3}/(\tau/\mu_{2})
.
Hence:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\mu_{1}/\tau&=&\hat{\mu}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\hat{\mu}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\hat{\mu}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:generalized_flateq_perm},
as $\hat{\mu}_{1}\Leftrightarrow\hat{\mu}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\hat{\mu}_{3}$}\\
&=&(\mu_{2}/\tau)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\tau/\mu_{2}))\\
&=&(\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}/\tau)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}/(\tau/\mu_{2}))&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&=&(\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}^{\mathcal{F}})/\tau\end{array}

The projection of a rewrite over itself is always empty;
specifically
\rho/\rho\mathrel{\sim}\rho^{\blacktriangleright}
where, recall from above,
\rho^{\blacktriangleright}=(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})^{\mathcal{F}}
.

Lemma 50

(Self-erasure)
.

\rho/\rho\mathrel{\sim}\rho^{\blacktriangleright}

Proof 6.14
.

By induction on
\rho
.
If
\rho=\mu
is a multistep, then
\mu/\mu=\mu^{\blacktriangleright}

by Proposition
40
.
If
\rho=\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}
then:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}&&(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})/(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})\\
&=&((\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})/\rho_{1})/\rho_{2}\\
&=&((\rho_{1}/\rho_{1})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1})))/\rho_{2}&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:projrr_sequence}}\\
&=&((\rho_{1}/\rho_{1})/\rho_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1}))/(\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1})))&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:projrr_sequence}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&(\rho_{1}^{\blacktriangleright}/\rho_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1}))/(\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1})))&\text{by IH and Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&=&(\rho_{2}^{\blacktriangleleft}/\rho_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1}))/(\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1})))&\text{as $\rho_{1}^{\mathsf{tgt}}=_{\beta\eta}\rho_{2}^{\mathsf{src}}$}\\
&=&\rho_{2}^{\blacktriangleright}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1}))/(\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1})))&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:basic_properties_of_rewrite_projection}}\\
&=&(\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1}))/(\rho_{2}/(\rho_{1}/\rho_{1}))&\text{by {{$\mathrel{\approx}$-IdL}} and Theorem~\ref{thm:body:soundness_completeness_flateq}}\\
&=&(\rho_{2}/\rho_{1}^{\blacktriangleright})/(\rho_{2}/\rho_{1}^{\blacktriangleright})&\text{by IH and Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&=&(\rho_{2}/\rho_{2}^{\blacktriangleleft})/(\rho_{2}/\rho_{2}^{\blacktriangleleft})&\text{as $\rho_{1}^{\mathsf{tgt}}=_{\beta\eta}\rho_{2}^{\mathsf{src}}$}\\
&=&\rho_{2}/\rho_{2}&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:basic_properties_of_rewrite_projection}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\rho_{2}^{\blacktriangleright}&\text{by IH }\\
&=&(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})^{\blacktriangleright}&\end{array}

Finally, an important property is that

\rho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma/\rho)\mathrel{\sim}\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho/\sigma)
,
corresponding to a strong form of confluence. Its proof makes use of Lemma
51
below.

Lemma 51

(Multistep permutation)
.

Let
\mu,\nu
be coinitial multisteps.
Then:

\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu/\mu)\mathrel{\approx}\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/\nu)

Proof 6.15
.

Recall that if
\mu^{\mathcal{F}}=(\mu^{\prime})^{\mathcal{F}}

and
\nu^{\mathcal{F}}=(\nu^{\prime})^{\mathcal{F}}
,
then
\mu/\nu=\mu^{\prime}/\nu^{\prime}

by Proposition
40
.
Moreover, by Lemma
31

if
\mu
is a step and
\mu^{\prime}
is its
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal
form, then
\mu\mathrel{\approx}\mu^{\prime}
.
Hence, to prove the statement of the lemma,
it suffices to show that

\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu/\mu)\mathrel{\approx}\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/\nu)

assuming that
\mu,\nu
are coinitial multisteps
in
\mathrel{\overset{\circ}{\mapsto}},\overline{\eta}
-normal form.

The proof proceeds by induction on the number of applications
in
\mu
, and by case analysis on the head of
\mu
.
We provide a sample case, namely when
\mu
headed by a constant. The others
are developed similarly. The case where
\mu
is headed by a rule symbol
makes use of the fact that the HRS is orthogonal.

Suppose, therefore, that the
\mu
headed by a constant.
Then
\mu=\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{n}
.
Since
\mu
and
\nu
are coinitial, the head of
\nu

can be either
\mathbf{c}
or a rule symbol
\varrho
.
We consider two subcases:

1.

\nu
headed by a constant.

Then
\nu=\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,\nu_{1}\ldots\nu_{n}
.
Note that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}&&\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu/\mu)\\
&=&(\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{n})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,\nu_{1}\ldots\nu_{n})/(\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{n}))\\
&=&(\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{n})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,(\nu_{1}/\mu_{1})\ldots(\nu_{n}/\mu_{n}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&\mathrel{\approx}&(\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,\mu_{1}\ldots\mu_{n})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,(\nu_{1}/\mu_{1})\ldots(\nu_{n}/\mu_{n}))&\text{by~Lemma~\ref{lemma:flattening_sound_wrt_permeq}}\\
&\mathrel{\approx}&\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,(\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{1}/\mu_{1}))\ldots(\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{n}/\mu_{n}))&\text{by~{{$\mathrel{\approx}$-Abs}}, {{$\mathrel{\approx}$-App}}}\end{array}

Similarly:

\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/\nu)\mathrel{\approx}\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,(\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{1}/\nu_{1}))\ldots(\nu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{n}/\nu_{n}))

Moreover, by IH we have that

\mu_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{i}/\mu_{i})\mathrel{\approx}\nu_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{i}/\nu_{i})

for all
1\leq i\leq n
, so:

\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,(\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{1}/\mu_{1}))\ldots(\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{n}/\mu_{n}))\mathrel{\approx}\lambda\vec{x}.\mathbf{c}\,(\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{1}/\nu_{1}))\ldots(\nu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{n}/\nu_{n}))

2.

\nu
headed by a rule symbol.

Then
\nu=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,\nu_{1}\ldots\nu_{q}
.
By Lemma
45

we have that

\mu_{1}=(\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\,\mu^{\prime}_{1}\ldots\mu^{\prime}_{q})^{\circ}

where
\mu^{\prime}_{i}
and
\nu_{i}
are coinitial
for all
1\leq i\leq q
,
and each
\mu^{\prime}_{i}
has strictly less applications than
\mu
.
Note that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}&&\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu/\mu)\\
&=&(\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\,\mu^{\prime}_{1}\ldots\mu^{\prime}_{q})^{\circ}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,\nu_{1}\ldots\nu_{q})/(\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\,\mu^{\prime}_{1}\ldots\mu^{\prime}_{q})^{\circ})\\
&=&(\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{<\overline{\eta}}}\,\mu^{\prime}_{1}\ldots\mu^{\prime}_{q})^{\circ}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\lambda\vec{x}.(\varrho/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}})(\nu_{1}/\mu^{\prime}_{1})\ldots(\nu_{q}/\mu^{\prime}_{q}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&\mathrel{\approx}&(\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}\,\mu^{\prime}_{1}\ldots\mu^{\prime}_{q})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\lambda\vec{x}.(\varrho/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}})(\nu_{1}/\mu^{\prime}_{1})\ldots(\nu_{q}/\mu^{\prime}_{q}))&\text{by~Lemma~\ref{lemma:flattening_sound_wrt_permeq}}\\
&\mathrel{\approx}&(\lambda\vec{x}.(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\varrho/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}))(\mu^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{1}/\mu^{\prime}_{1}))\ldots(\mu^{\prime}_{q}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{q}/\mu^{\prime}_{q})))&\text{by~{{$\mathrel{\approx}$-Abs}}, {{$\mathrel{\approx}$-App}}}\end{array}

Similarly:

\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/\nu)\mathrel{\approx}(\lambda\vec{x}.(\varrho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}/\varrho))(\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{1}/\nu_{1}))\ldots(\nu_{q}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{q}/\nu_{q})))

Note that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\varrho/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}})&=&\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&=&\varrho&\text{by~{{$\mathrel{\approx}$-IdL}}}\\
&=&\varrho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}&\text{by~{{$\mathrel{\approx}$-IdR}}}\\
&\mathrel{\approx}&\varrho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho^{\blacktriangleright}&\text{by~Lemma~\ref{lemma:flattening_sound_wrt_permeq}}\\
&=&\varrho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}/\varrho)\end{array}

Moreover, by IH we know that

\mu^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{i}/\mu^{\prime}_{i})\mathrel{\approx}\nu_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{i}/\nu_{i})

for all
1\leq i\leq q
.
Hence:

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\lambda\vec{x}.(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\varrho/\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}))(\mu^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{1}/\mu^{\prime}_{1}))\ldots(\mu^{\prime}_{q}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{q}/\mu^{\prime}_{q}))\\
\mathrel{\approx}&\lambda\vec{x}.(\varrho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\varrho^{\mathsf{src}}/\varrho))(\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{1}/\nu_{1}))\ldots(\nu_{q}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{q}/\nu_{q}))\end{array}

Lemma 52

(Rewrite permutation)
.

If
\rho,\sigma
are coinitial flat rewrites then:

\rho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma/\rho)\mathrel{\sim}\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho/\sigma)

Proof 6.16
.

We proceed by induction on
\rho
:

1.

Multistep,
\rho=\mu
.

To prove

\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma/\mu)\mathrel{\sim}\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/\sigma)

we proceed by a nested induction on
\sigma
:

1.1

Multistep,
\sigma=\nu
.

By Lemma
51

we have that

\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu/\mu)\mathrel{\approx}\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/\nu)
.
By Theorem
36

this implies that

\mu^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu/\mu)^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{\sim}\nu^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/\nu)^{\mathcal{F}}
.
But
\mu
and
\nu
are flat,
and by Proposition
40
we know that

\mu/\nu=(\mu/\nu)^{\mathcal{F}}

and

\nu/\mu=(\nu/\mu)^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Hence

\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu/\mu)\mathrel{\sim}\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/\nu)
.

1.2

Composition,
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2}
.

To alleviate the notation,
we work implicitly modulo the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Assoc
rule.
Note that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2})/\mu)&=&\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma_{1}/\mu)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma_{2}/(\mu/\sigma_{1}))\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/\sigma_{1})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma_{2}/(\mu/\sigma_{1}))&\text{by IH }\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\mu/\sigma_{1})/\sigma_{2})&\text{by IH }\\
&=&\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu/(\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2}))\end{array}

2.

Composition,
\rho=\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}
.

To alleviate the notation,
we work implicitly modulo the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Assoc
rule.
Note that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma/(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}))&=&\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\sigma/\rho_{1})/\rho_{2})\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma/\rho_{1})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/(\sigma/\rho_{1}))&\text{by IH }\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{1}/\sigma)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}/(\sigma/\rho_{1}))&\text{by IH }\\
&=&\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})/\sigma)&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:projrr_sequence}}\end{array}

6.3.
Projection for Arbitrary Rewrites.

As a final step, the projection operator of Definition
6.2

may be extended to arbitrary rewrites by flattening first.

{defi}

[Projection operator for arbitrary rewrites]

Let
\rho,\sigma
be arbitrary coinitial rewrites.
Their projection is defined as

\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}\,\rho^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}
.

We next address some basic properties of projection over arbitrary rewrites (Proposition
55
) including that permutation equivalence is a congruence with respect to it. Since
\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma
is defined in terms of projection over flat rewrites, we first prove some auxiliary results on that operator, namely Lemma
53
and Lemma
54
.

Lemma 53

(Projection of abstraction)
.

The following hold:

1.

If
\mu
is a flat multistep and
\rho
is a coinitial flat rewrite,
then

(\lambda x.\mu)^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\lambda x.\rho)^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x.(\mu\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho))^{\mathcal{F}}
.

2.

If
\rho
is a flat rewrite and
\mu
is a coinitial flat multistep,
then

(\lambda x.\rho)^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{2}}(\lambda x.\mu)^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x.(\rho\mathrel{/^{2}}\mu))^{\mathcal{F}}
.

3.

If
\rho,\sigma
are coinitial flat rewrites,
then

(\lambda x.\rho)^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{3}}(\lambda x.\sigma)^{\mathcal{F}}=(\lambda x.(\rho\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma))^{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof 6.17
.

The first item is by induction on
\rho
using Proposition
40
and Proposition
29
. The second is similar and also uses the first item. The third is by induction on
\sigma
and uses Proposition
29
and the second item.

Lemma 54

(Projection of application)
.

The following hold:

1.

If
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
are flat multisteps and

\rho_{1},\rho_{2}
are flat rewrites
such that
\mu_{1}
and
\rho_{1}
are coinitial
and
\mu_{2}
and
\rho_{2}
are coinitial,
then

(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{1}\,\rho_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}=((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{1})\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{2}))^{\mathcal{F}}
.

2.

If
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}
are flat rewrites and

\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
are flat multisteps
such that
\rho_{1}
and
\mu_{1}
are coinitial
and
\rho_{2}
and
\mu_{2}
are coinitial,
then

(\rho_{1}\,\rho_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{2}}(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}=((\rho_{1}\mathrel{/^{2}}\mu_{1})\,(\rho_{2}\mathrel{/^{2}}\mu_{2}))^{\mathcal{F}}
.

3.

If
\rho_{1},\rho_{2},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
are flat rewrites
such that
\rho_{1}
and
\sigma_{1}
are coinitial
and
\rho_{2}
and
\sigma_{2}
are coinitial,
then

(\rho_{1}\,\rho_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{3}}(\sigma_{1}\,\sigma_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}=((\rho_{1}\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma_{1})\,(\rho_{2}\mathrel{/^{3}}\sigma_{2}))^{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof 6.18
.

All items use the fact that
\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}
is strongly normalizing (Proposition
28
),
and proceed by induction on the length of the longest reduction of their target. We present the proof of the first item as a sample.
We proceed by induction on the length of the longest reduction

\rho_{1}\,\rho_{2}\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}(\rho_{1}\,\rho_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}
,
considering four cases, depending on whether
each of
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
is a multistep or a composition:

1.

If both are multisteps,
i.e.
\rho_{1}=\nu_{1}
and
\rho_{2}=\nu_{2}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\nu_{1}\,\nu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}&=&(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}/(\nu_{1}\,\nu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})/(\nu_{1}\,\nu_{2}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}/\nu_{1})\,(\mu_{2}/\nu_{2}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}\nu_{1})\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}\nu_{2}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\end{array}

2.

If
\rho_{1}=\nu_{1}
is a multistep
and
\rho_{2}=\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}
is a sequence:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\nu_{1}\,(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
&=&(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}((\nu_{1}\,\rho_{21})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{1}^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,\rho_{22})^{\mathcal{F}})&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:flat_confluent}}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\nu_{1}\,\rho_{21})^{\mathcal{F}})\mathrel{/^{1}}(\nu_{1}^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,\rho_{22})^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}\nu_{1})\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{21}))^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\nu_{1}^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,\rho_{22})^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by IH }\\
&=&(((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}\nu_{1})\mathrel{/^{1}}\nu_{1}^{\mathsf{tgt}})\,((\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{21})\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{22}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by IH }\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu_{1}^{\mathsf{tgt}}))\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22})))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu_{1}^{\blacktriangleright}))\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22})))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:flat_confluent}}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}\nu_{1}))\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22})))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}, since $\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu_{1}^{\blacktriangleright}\mathrel{\sim}\nu_{1}$}\end{array}

To justify that the IH may be applied, note that

\nu_{1}\,(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22})\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}(\nu_{1}\,\rho_{21})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\nu_{1}^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,\rho_{22})
.

3.

If
\rho_{1}=\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}
is a sequence
and
\rho_{2}=\nu_{2}
is a multistep, the proof is similar
as for the previous case.

4.

If both are sequences, i.e.
\rho_{1}=\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}

and
\rho_{2}=\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}((\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12})\,(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}))^{\mathcal{F}}\\
&=&(\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}(((\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12})\,\rho_{21}^{\mathsf{src}}))^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{12}^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}))^{\mathcal{F}})&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:flat_confluent}}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\,\mu_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}((\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12})\,\rho_{21}^{\mathsf{src}}))^{\mathcal{F}})\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{12}^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}))\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{21}^{\mathsf{src}}))^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{12}^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by IH }\\
&=&(((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}))\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{12}^{\mathsf{tgt}})\,((\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}\rho_{21}^{\mathsf{src}})\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22})))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by IH }\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}((\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}^{\mathsf{tgt}}))\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{21}^{\mathsf{src}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}))))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}((\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}^{\blacktriangleright}))\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{21}^{\blacktriangleleft}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}))))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by~Proposition~\ref{prop:flat_confluent}}\\
&=&((\mu_{1}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}))\,(\mu_{2}\mathrel{/^{1}}(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22})))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\end{array}

To justify that the IH may be applied, note that

(\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12})\,(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22})\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}(\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12})\,\rho_{21}^{\mathsf{src}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,(\rho_{21}\,\rho_{22})
.
To justify the last equality, note that

(\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}^{\blacktriangleright}\mathrel{\sim}\rho_{11}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{12}

and

\rho_{21}^{\blacktriangleleft}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22})\mathrel{\sim}\rho_{21}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{22}
.

Proposition 55

(Properties of projection for arbitrary rewrites)
.

1.

Projection of a rewrite over a sequence and of a sequence over a rewrite
obey the expected equations

\rho{/\!\!/}(\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2})=(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma_{1}){/\!\!/}\sigma_{2}

and

(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}){/\!\!/}\sigma=(\rho_{1}{/\!\!/}\sigma)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}{/\!\!/}(\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho_{1}))
.

2.

Projection commutes with abstraction and application, that is:

2.1

(\lambda x.\rho){/\!\!/}(\lambda x.\sigma)\mathrel{\approx}\lambda x.(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma)
,
and more precisely

(\lambda x.\rho){/\!\!/}(\lambda x.\sigma)\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsfrom}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}\lambda x.(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma)
.

2.2

If
\rho_{1},\sigma_{1}
are coinitial and
\rho_{2},\sigma_{2}
are coinitial,
then

(\rho_{1}\,\rho_{2}){/\!\!/}(\sigma_{1}\,\sigma_{2})\mathrel{\approx}(\rho_{1}{/\!\!/}\sigma_{1})\,(\rho_{2}{/\!\!/}\sigma_{2})
,
and more precisely

(\rho_{1}\,\rho_{2}){/\!\!/}(\sigma_{1}\,\sigma_{2})\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsfrom}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}(\rho_{1}{/\!\!/}\sigma_{1})\,(\rho_{2}{/\!\!/}\sigma_{2})
.

3.

The projection of a rewrite over itself is always empty,

\rho{/\!\!/}\rho\mathrel{\approx}\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}}
.

4.

Permutation equivalence is a congruence with respect to projection,
namely if
\rho\mathrel{\approx}\sigma

then
\tau{/\!\!/}\rho=\tau{/\!\!/}\sigma

and
\rho{/\!\!/}\tau\mathrel{\approx}\sigma{/\!\!/}\tau
.

5.

The key equation

\rho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho)\mathrel{\approx}\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma)

holds.

Proof 6.19
.

We prove each item separately:

1.

Projection of composition:

•

On one hand:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho{/\!\!/}(\sigma_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2})&=&\rho^{\mathcal{F}}/(\sigma_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma_{2}^{\mathcal{F}})&\text{by definition}\\
&=&(\rho^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma_{1}^{\mathcal{F}})/\sigma_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\\
&=&(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma_{1})/\sigma_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&=&(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma_{1})^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{as $\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma_{1}$ is flat by construction}\\
&=&(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma_{1}){/\!\!/}\sigma_{2}&\text{by definition}\end{array}

•

On the other hand:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}){/\!\!/}\sigma&=&(\rho_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&=&(\rho_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho_{2}^{\mathcal{F}})/\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}\\
&=&(\rho_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma^{\mathcal{F}})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}/(\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}/\rho_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}))\\
&=&(\rho_{1}{/\!\!/}\sigma)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}/(\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho_{1}))&\text{by definition}\\
&=&(\rho_{1}{/\!\!/}\sigma)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}/(\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho_{1})^{\mathcal{F}})&\text{as $\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho_{1}$ is flat by construction}\\
&=&(\rho_{1}{/\!\!/}\sigma)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho_{2}{/\!\!/}(\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho_{1}))&\text{by definition}\end{array}

2.

Projection commutes with

2.1

Abstraction:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}(\lambda x.\rho){/\!\!/}(\lambda x.\sigma)&=&(\lambda x.\rho)^{\mathcal{F}}/(\lambda x.\sigma)^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&=&(\lambda x.\rho^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}/(\lambda x.\sigma^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:flat_confluent}}\\
&=&(\lambda x.(\rho^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:proja_abstraction}}\\
&\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsfrom}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}&\lambda x.(\rho^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma^{\mathcal{F}})\\
&=&\lambda x.(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma)&\text{by definition}\end{array}

2.2

Application:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}(\rho_{1}\,\rho_{2}){/\!\!/}(\sigma_{1}\,\sigma_{2})&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&(\rho_{1}\,\rho_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}/(\sigma_{1}\,\sigma_{2})^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&(\rho_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\,\rho_{2}^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}/(\sigma_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\,\sigma_{2}^{\mathcal{F}})^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:flat_confluent}}\\
&\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}&((\rho_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma_{1}^{\mathcal{F}})\,(\rho_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}))^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:proja_application}}\\
&\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsfrom}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}&(\rho_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma_{1}^{\mathcal{F}})\,(\rho_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma_{2}^{\mathcal{F}})\\
&=&(\rho_{1}{/\!\!/}\sigma_{1})\,(\rho_{2}{/\!\!/}\sigma_{2})&\text{by definition}\end{array}

3.

Self-erasure:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho{/\!\!/}\rho&=&\rho^{\mathcal{F}}/\rho^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&(\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}})^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:self_erasure}}\\
&\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsfrom}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}&\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}}\end{array}

It suffices to recall that
flat permutation equivalence (
\mathrel{\sim}
) and
flattening (
\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}
)
are both included in permutation equivalence (
\mathrel{\approx}
).

4.

Congruence of projection:
Let
\rho\mathrel{\approx}\sigma
.
By Theorem
36
this means that

\rho^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{\sim}\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}
. Then:

•

On one hand:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\tau{/\!\!/}\rho&=&\tau^{\mathcal{F}}/\rho^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&=&\tau^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&=&\tau{/\!\!/}\sigma&\text{by definition}\end{array}

•

On the other hand:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho{/\!\!/}\tau&=&\rho^{\mathcal{F}}/\tau^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by definition}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}/\tau^{\mathcal{F}}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&=&\sigma{/\!\!/}\tau&\text{by definition}\end{array}

5.

Permutation:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho)&=&\rho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}/\rho^{\mathcal{F}})&\text{by definition}\\
&\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}&\rho^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}/\rho^{\mathcal{F}})\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma^{\mathcal{F}})&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:rewrite_permutation}}\\
&\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsfrom}{\hskip-1.99997pt}^{*}}&\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho^{\mathcal{F}}/\sigma^{\mathcal{F}})\\
&=&\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma)&\text{by definition}\end{array}

It suffices to recall that
flat permutation equivalence (
\mathrel{\sim}
) and
flattening (
\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}
)
are both included in permutation equivalence (
\mathrel{\approx}
).

6.4.
Characterization of Permutation Equivalence in Terms of Projection.

Finally, we are able to characterize permutation equivalence

\rho\mathrel{\approx}\sigma
as the condition that the projections

\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma
and
\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho
are both empty.
Indeed:

Theorem 56

(Projection equivalence)
.

Let
\rho,\sigma
be arbitrary coinitial rewrites.
Then
\rho\mathrel{\approx}\sigma

if and only if

\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma\mathrel{\approx}\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}}

and

\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho\mathrel{\approx}\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}}
.

Proof 6.20
.

(\Rightarrow)

Suppose that
\rho\mathrel{\approx}\sigma
.
Then, by Proposition
55
,

\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma\mathrel{\approx}\sigma{/\!\!/}\sigma\mathrel{\approx}\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}}
.
Symmetrically,
\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho\mathrel{\approx}\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}}
.

(\Leftarrow)

Let
\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma\mathrel{\approx}\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}}

and
\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho\mathrel{\approx}\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}}
.
Then, by Proposition
55
,

\rho\,\,\,\mathrel{\approx}\,\,\,\rho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,\,\,\mathrel{\approx}\,\,\,\rho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\sigma{/\!\!/}\rho)\,\,\,\mathrel{\approx}\,\,\,\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma)\,\,\,\mathrel{\approx}\,\,\,\sigma\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,\,\,\mathrel{\approx}\,\,\,\sigma
.

Since flattening and projection are computable,
Theorem
36
and Theorem
56

together provide an
effective method to decide permutation equivalence

\rho\mathrel{\approx}\sigma
for arbitrary rewrites.
Indeed, to test whether

\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma\mathrel{\approx}\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}}
,
note by Theorem
36

that this is equivalent to testing whether

\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma\mathrel{\sim}(\sigma^{\mathsf{tgt}})^{\mathcal{F}}
,
so it suffices to check that
\rho{/\!\!/}\sigma
is
empty
,

i.e.
it contains no rule symbols.
This is justified by the fact that if
\mu
has no rule symbols and

\mu\mathrel{\sim}\rho
, then
\rho
has no rule symbols.

Lemma 57

(Characterization of empty multisteps)
.

Let
\mu
be a flat multistep.
Then the following are equivalent:

1.

\mu
is a term, i.e.
\mu=\underline{s}
.

2.

There exists a term
s
such that
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\underline{s}
.

3.

There exists a term
s
such that if

\mu\mathrel{\sim}\rho

then there exists a composition context
\mathtt{K}

such that

\rho=\mathtt{K}\langle\underline{s},\ldots,\underline{s}\rangle
.

4.

There exists a term
s
such that if

\mu\mathrel{\sim}\nu
then
\nu=\underline{s}
.

Proof 6.21
.

•

(1\implies 2)

Let
\mu=\underline{s}
.
Then it is immediate as
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\underline{s}
.

•

(2\implies 3)

Let
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\underline{s}

and suppose that
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\rho
.
We claim that there is a composition context
\mathtt{K}
such that

\rho=\mathtt{K}\langle\underline{s},\ldots,\underline{s}\rangle
.
First, note that we have that
\underline{s}\mathrel{\sim}\rho
.
This means that there is a sequence of applications of the axioms
defining flat permutation equivalence such that

\underline{s}=\rho_{0}\mathrel{\sim}\rho_{1}\mathrel{\sim}\rho_{2}\ldots\mathrel{\sim}\rho_{n}=\rho
.
We proceed by induction on
n
.
If
n=0
, it is trivial taking
\mathtt{K}:=\Box
.
For the inductive step, it suffices to show
that if
\rho_{i}
is of the form

\rho_{i}=\mathtt{K}\langle\underline{s},\ldots,\underline{s}\rangle

then
\rho_{i+1}
is of the form

\rho_{i}=\mathtt{K}^{\prime}\langle\underline{s},\ldots,\underline{s}\rangle
.
The case for the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Assoc
rule is immediate.
The interesting case is the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
rule.
There are two subcases, depending on whether the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm

rule is applied forwards or backwards:

1.

Forwards application of
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
.

That is,
\rho_{i}=\mathtt{S}\langle\underline{s}\rangle

and
\rho_{i+1}=\mathtt{S}\langle\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\rangle

where
\mathtt{S}
is a composition context
and
\underline{s}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}
.
Given that
s
has no rule symbols, it is easy to check by
induction on
s

that
\mu_{1}=\underline{s}
and
\mu_{2}=\underline{s}
.
This concludes the proof of this case.

2.

Backwards application of
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
.

That is,
\rho_{i}=\mathtt{S}\langle\underline{s}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}\rangle

and
\rho_{i+1}=\mathtt{S}\langle\mu_{1}\rangle

where
\mu\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

and
\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}=\underline{s}
.
Note that
\mu_{1}
is a flat multistep.
To finish the proof,
it suffices to show that,

\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}=\mu_{3}
.
This is implied by the following claim.

Claim.

Let
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

where
\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}=\underline{s}

for some term
s
.
Then
\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}=\mu_{3}
.

Proof of the claim.
We proceed by induction on
\mu_{1}
, following the characterization of

\mathrel{\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\mapsto}}
-normal multisteps,
There are three subcases, depending on the head of
\mu_{1}
:

2.1

\mu_{1}
headed by a variable.

Then

\mu_{1}=\lambda\vec{x}.x\,\nu_{11}\ldots\nu_{1n}
.
The judgment
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

must be derived by a number of
applications of the
SAbs
rule,
followed by
n
applications of the
SApp
rule,
followed by an application of the
SVar
rule.
Hence

\mu_{2}=\lambda\vec{x}.x\,\nu_{21}\ldots\nu_{2n}

and

\mu_{3}=\lambda\vec{x}.x\,\nu_{31}\ldots\nu_{3n}
,
where moreover
\nu_{1i}\Leftrightarrow\nu_{2i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu_{3i}

for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Since
\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{3}^{\mathcal{F}}

then also
\mu_{2i}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{3i}^{\mathcal{F}}

for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
and moreover
\mu_{2i}^{\mathcal{F}}
must be a term
(i.e. without occurrences of rule symbols),
for otherwise there would be a rule symbol in
\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then by IH we have that

\mu_{1i}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{2i}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{3i}^{\mathcal{F}}

for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
This concludes the proof.

2.2

\mu_{1}
headed by a constant.

Similar to the previous case, when
\mu_{1}
is headed by a variable.

2.3

\mu_{1}
headed by a rule symbol.

We argue that this case is impossible.
Indeed, if

\mu_{1}
is of the form

\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,\nu_{11}\ldots\nu_{1n}

then the judgment
\mu_{1}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

must be derived by a number of
applications of the
SAbs
rule,
followed by
n
applications of the
SApp
rule,
followed by an application of either
SRuleL

or
SRuleR
at the head.
If the judgment is derived
by an application of
SRuleL
at the head,
then we have that

\mu_{2}=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho\,\nu_{21}\ldots\nu_{2n}

and

\mu_{3}=\lambda\vec{x}.\varrho^{\mathsf{tgt}}\,\nu_{31}\ldots\nu_{3n}

and moreover that

\mu_{1i}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3i}

for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
But this is impossible, given that
\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}
would contain
a rule symbol, contradicting the fact that

\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=\underline{s}
.
Using a similar argument, we note that the judgment
cannot be derived by an application of the
SRuleR
rule
at the head.

•

(3\implies 4)

Suppose that there exists a term
s

such that if
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\rho

then
\rho
is of the form

\rho=\mathtt{K}\langle\underline{s},\ldots,\underline{s}\rangle
.
Moreover, suppose that
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\nu
.
Then by hypothesis
\nu
must be of the form

\mathtt{K}\langle\underline{s},\ldots,\underline{s}\rangle
.
Since
\nu
is a multistep, containing no compositions,
then
\mathtt{K}\langle=\rangle\Box
and indeed
\nu=\underline{s}
.

•

(4\implies 1)

Suppose that there exists a term
s

such that if
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\nu

then
\nu=\underline{s}
.
Then in particular, since
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\mu
,
we have that
\mu=\underline{s}
.

7.
Standardization

A rewrite is considered
standard
if the steps of the computation
are ordered in such a way that they cannot be moved further upfront.
The process that reorders steps, converting rewrites into their standard
forms, is called
standardization
.
Any two rewrites that convert to the same
standard rewrite are considered
standardization equivalent
.
In this section we introduce the

standardization rewrite system
(Definition
7
),
we show that it is

strongly normalizing and confluent
(Proposition
59
),
subject to a finiteness condition
and up to a notion of strong equivalence.
From this we derive
a
standardization result
(Theorem
60
).

To formulate
standardization, it is convenient to deal with sequential
rewrites,
consisting of a sequence of composed flat multisteps
ending in a unit rewrite,

i.e.
rewrites of the form

\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}
with
n\geq 0
.
Composition (“
\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}
”) is assumed to be right-associative.
The
length
of a sequential rewrite is

\ell(\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s})\,\mathrel{\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}}\,n
.
Each flat rewrite is equivalent to some (non-unique)
sequential rewrite, by associating compositions,
and using the equivalence
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\mathsf{tgt}})^{\mathcal{F}}
to
ensure that the last multistep is empty.

{defi}

[Standardization rewrite system]

The standardization rewrite system
is given by the set of all sequential rewrites,
with a reduction rule
\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\sigma
defined by:
