\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 13.15627pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=26.31253pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsf{{{{$\rhd$-{Del}}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{s}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\rho$}}}}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\mu_{12}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\hskip 14.22636pt\mu_{23}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\hskip 14.22636pt\mu_{2}\text{\ is non-empty}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=193.67307pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textsf{{{{$\rhd$-{Pull}}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 56.37927pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho$}}}}\end{array}

and closed by a congruence rule
\rhd
-Cong

stating that
\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\sigma

implies
\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\sigma
.

Rule
\rhd
-Del
removes empty steps from the rewrite
(other than the very last one). Rule
\rhd
-Pull
“pulls”
steps upfront.

{exa}

Given typed constants
\mathbf{d}:(\iota\to\iota)\to\iota

and
\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}:\iota\to\iota
,
and rewriting rules

\varrho:\lambda x.\lambda y.\mathbf{d}\,(\lambda z.x\,z)\,y\rightarrowtriangle\lambda x.\lambda y.x\,(x\,y):(\iota\to\iota)\to\iota\to\iota

and

\vartheta:\lambda x.\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{f}(x))\rightarrowtriangle\lambda x.\mathbf{g}(x):\iota\to\iota
,
note first that:

\begin{array}[]{l@{\HS\HS}l}\mathbf{d}(\varrho\,\mathbf{f}):\mathbf{d}\,(\mathbf{d}\,\mathbf{f})\rightarrowtriangle\mathbf{d}\,(\lambda x.\mathbf{f}\,(\mathbf{f}\,x))\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt&\mathbf{d}(\vartheta):\mathbf{d}\,(\lambda x.\mathbf{f}\,(\mathbf{f}\,x))\rightarrowtriangle\mathbf{d}\,\mathbf{g}\\
\varrho\,\mathbf{g}:\mathbf{d}\,\mathbf{g}\rightarrowtriangle\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,(\mathbf{g}\,x)\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt&\varrho\,(\varrho\,\mathbf{f}):\mathbf{d}\,(\mathbf{d}\,\mathbf{f})\rightarrowtriangle\lambda x.\mathbf{f}\,(\mathbf{f}\,(\mathbf{f}\,(\mathbf{f}\,x)))\\
\varrho\,\vartheta:\mathbf{d}\,(\lambda x.\mathbf{f}\,(\mathbf{f}\,x))\rightarrowtriangle\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,(\mathbf{g}\,x)\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt&\lambda x.\vartheta\,(\vartheta\,x):\lambda x.\mathbf{f}\,(\mathbf{f}\,(\mathbf{f}\,(\mathbf{f}\,x)))\rightarrowtriangle\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,(\mathbf{g}\,x)\\
\end{array}

Moreover:

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\mathbf{d}(\varrho\,\mathbf{f})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mathbf{d}(\vartheta)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho\,\mathbf{g}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,(\mathbf{g}\,x)}\\
\mathrel{\rhd}^{(\footnotesize{\textsf{{Pull}}})}&\mathbf{d}(\varrho\,\mathbf{f})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho\,\vartheta\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,(\mathbf{g}\,x)}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,(\mathbf{g}\,x)}\\
\mathrel{\rhd}^{(\footnotesize{\textsf{{Del}}})}&\mathbf{d}(\varrho\,\mathbf{f})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\varrho\,\vartheta\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,(\mathbf{g}\,x)}\\
\mathrel{\rhd}^{(\footnotesize{\textsf{{Pull}}})}&\varrho\,(\varrho\,\mathbf{f})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\lambda x.\vartheta\,(\vartheta\,x)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{\lambda x.\mathbf{g}\,(\mathbf{g}\,x)}\end{array}

One important observation in the presence of erasing rewrite rules is
that

\mu\mathrel{\sim}\nu

does not necessarily imply that
\mu=\nu
.
For example, if

\varrho:\lambda x.\mathbf{c}\,x\to\lambda x.\mathbf{d}

and

\vartheta:\mathbf{e}\to\mathbf{e}^{\prime}

then the multistep

\varrho\,\vartheta:\mathbf{c}\,\mathbf{e}\to\mathbf{d}

and the multistep

\varrho\,\underline{\mathbf{e}}:\mathbf{c}\,\mathbf{e}\to\mathbf{d}

are permutation equivalent.
To justify this, note that, on one hand,

\varrho\,\vartheta\Leftrightarrow\varrho\,\underline{\mathbf{e}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\lambda x.\underline{\mathbf{d}})\,\vartheta

and, on the other hand,

\varrho\,\underline{\mathbf{e}}\Leftrightarrow\varrho\,\underline{\mathbf{e}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\lambda x.\underline{\mathbf{d}})\,\underline{\mathbf{e}}

so, by the
\mathcal{F}
-Perm
rule,

\varrho\,\vartheta\mathrel{\sim}\varrho\,\underline{\mathbf{e}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{\mathbf{d}}\mathrel{\sim}\varrho\,\underline{\mathbf{e}}
.
Thus, in the standardization system that we propose,
uniqueness of standardization will be determined
modulo a notion of
strong equivalence

rather than syntactical equality of the resulting sequential rewrites.

{defi}

[Strong equivalence]
Two sequential rewrites
\rho,\sigma

are said to be
strongly equivalent
,
written
\rho\mathrel{\asymp}\sigma
,
if they are of the form

\rho=\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}

and

\sigma=\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}

where
\mu_{i}\mathrel{\sim}\nu_{i}
for all
i\in 1..n
.

It can then be shown that standardization is locally confluent,
up to strong equivalence:

Lemma 58

(Local confluence of
\mathrel{\rhd}
up to
\mathrel{\asymp}
)
.

1.

Strong Bisimulation.

If
\rho\mathrel{\asymp}\rho^{\prime}\mathrel{\rhd}\sigma

then there exists
\sigma^{\prime}
such that

\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\sigma^{\prime}\mathrel{\asymp}\sigma
.

2.

Local Confluence.

If
\rho_{1}\mathrel{\rhd}\rho_{2}

and
\rho_{1}\mathrel{\rhd}\rho_{3}

then
\rho_{2}\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\mathrel{\asymp}\rho_{4}

and
\rho_{3}\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\mathrel{\asymp}\rho_{4}

for some
\rho_{4}
.

Proof 7.1
.

The proof of the first item is by induction on the derivation of
\rho^{\prime}\mathrel{\rhd}\sigma
. It relies on the property that
\mu=\underline{s}
iff
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\underline{s}
. For the second item, it suffices to study the cases in which the rewriting rules

\rhd
-Del
and
\rhd
-Pull
overlap. We present one sample case, the most interesting one, namely the
\rhd
-Pull
/
\rhd
-Pull
case.
Consider a
\rhd
-Pull
step

\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho

where
\mu_{12}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}

and
\mu_{23}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

with
\mu_{2}
a non-empty multistep.
Moreover, suppose that there is
another
\rhd
-Pull
step at the root,
of the form

\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho

where
\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{2}

and
\mu_{23}\mathrel{\sim}\mu^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}
,
with
\mu^{\prime}_{2}
a non-empty multistep.

Note that

\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{23}\mathrel{\sim}\mu^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}
.
Hence:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\mu_{3}&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{2}^{\blacktriangleright}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}&\text{by Theorem~\ref{thm:body:soundness_completeness_flateq} using {{$\mathrel{\approx}$-IdL}}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{2}^{\blacktriangleright}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/\mu_{2}^{\blacktriangleright})&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&(\mu_{2}/\mu_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\mu_{2}/\mu_{2}))&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:self_erasure} and Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&=&(\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3})/\mu_{2}&\text{by definition of projection}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&(\mu^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3})/\mu_{2}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&=&(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{3}/(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}))&\text{by definition of projection}\end{array}

Symmetrically,

\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{\sim}(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}))
.

The proof proceeds by case analysis, depending on whether

\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}
and
\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}

are empty or not. There are four subcases:

1.

\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}
and
\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}
are both empty.

Then, note that
\mu_{12}\mathrel{\sim}\mu^{\prime}_{12}

and
\mu_{3}\mathrel{\sim}\mu^{\prime}_{3}
. Indeed:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\mu_{12}&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{2}&\text{by Theorem~\ref{thm:body:projection_equivalence}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu^{\prime}_{12}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\mu_{3}&\mathrel{\sim}&(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{3}/(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}))&\text{as shown above}\\
&=&\mu_{2}^{\blacktriangleright}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{3}/(\mu^{\prime}_{2})^{\blacktriangleright})&\text{as $\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}$ is empty}\\
&\mathrel{\approx}&\mu^{\prime}_{3}&\text{by Theorem~\ref{thm:body:soundness_completeness_flateq},
Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\end{array}

Hence the diagram may be closed as follows:

\textstyle{\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\rhd
-Pull
\rhd
-Pull
\textstyle{\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho}
\textstyle{\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\asymp}}

2.

\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}
is empty and

\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}
is non-empty.

Let
\nu:=\mu_{12}
.
First note that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\nu&=&\mu_{12}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\blacktriangleright}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2})&\text{as $\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}$ is empty}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:multistep_permutation}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\end{array}

Moreover, note that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\mu^{\prime}_{3}&\mathrel{\sim}&(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}))&\text{as shown above}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/\mu_{2}^{\blacktriangleright})&\text{as $\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}$ is empty}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\end{array}

Since
\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}
is non-empty,
this means that there is a
\rhd
-Pull
step

\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho
.

Then:

\textstyle{\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\rhd
-Pull
\rhd
-Pull
\textstyle{\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\asymp}}
\textstyle{\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\rhd
-Pull
\textstyle{\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho}

3.

\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}
is non-empty and

\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}
is empty.

Symmetric to the previous case.

4.

\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}
and
\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}
are non-empty.

Let
\nu:=\mu_{12}\sqcup\mu^{\prime}_{12}

and
\xi:=\mu^{\prime}_{3}/(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})
.
We claim that there are two
\rhd
-Pull
steps,

\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho

and

\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho
.
We prove these two facts:

4.1

First, note that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\nu&=&\mu_{12}\sqcup\mu^{\prime}_{12}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{12}/\mu_{12})&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:rewrite_permutation}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{2})/(\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}))&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2})&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:self_erasure} and Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\end{array}

and recall that, as shown above

\mu_{3}\mathrel{\sim}(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{3}/(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}))=(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi
.
Hence, since
\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}
is non-empty,
there is a
\rhd
-Pull
step

\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho
.

4.2

Second, note that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\nu&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2})&\text{as already shown}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2})\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:rewrite_permutation}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\end{array}

Moreover, recall that, as shown above,

\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{\sim}(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}))
.
Therefore:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})/\mu^{\prime}_{3}&=&(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})/((\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu_{3}/(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2})))&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&=&((\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})/(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}))/(\mu_{3}/(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}))&\text{by definition of projection}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&(\mu^{\prime}_{2})^{\blacktriangleright}/(\mu_{3}/(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}))&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:self_erasure} and Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&(\mu_{3}/(\mu^{\prime}_{2}/\mu_{2}))^{\blacktriangleright}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\end{array}

This means that
(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})/\mu^{\prime}_{3}
is equivalent
to an empty multistep.
By Lemma
57
, this implies
that
(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})/\mu^{\prime}_{3}
itself has to be empty.
As a consequence, we have that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\mu^{\prime}_{3}&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}((\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})/\mu^{\prime}_{3})&\text{as $(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})/\mu^{\prime}_{3}$ is empty}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}(\mu^{\prime}_{3}/(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}))&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:rewrite_permutation}}\\
&=&(\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi\end{array}

Since
\mu_{2}/\mu^{\prime}_{2}
is non-empty,
there is a
\rhd
-Pull
step

\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho
.

With these two
\rhd
-Pull
steps, we are able to close the diagram:

\textstyle{\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\rhd
-Pull
\rhd
-Pull
\textstyle{\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\rhd
-Pull
\textstyle{\mu^{\prime}_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu^{\prime}_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\rhd
-Pull
\textstyle{\nu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho}

\rhd
-Pull
/
\rhd
-Pull
(2):
Consider two overlapping
\rhd
-Pull
steps,
the first one of the form

\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{45}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{45}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho

where

\mu_{12}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}

and

\mu_{23}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

with
\mu_{2}
a non-empty multistep,
and the second one of the form

\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{45}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{234}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho

where
\mu_{234}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{4}

and
\mu_{45}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{4}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}
,
with
\mu_{4}
a non-empty multistep.

Let
\mu_{34}:=\mu_{234}/\mu_{2}
.
We claim that there are two
\rhd
-Pull
steps,

\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{45}\mathrel{\rhd}\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{34}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}

and

\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{234}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}\mathrel{\rhd}\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{34}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}
.

1.

First, note that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\mu_{34}&=&\mu_{234}/\mu_{2}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&((\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3})\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{4})/\mu_{2}&\text{by Proposition~\ref{prop:congruence_flateq_projection}}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{4}&\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma:self_erasure} and Proposition~\ref{prop:body:properties_of_projection}}\end{array}

and recall that

\mu_{45}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{4}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}
.
Since
\mu_{4}
is non-empty,
this gives us a
\rhd
-Pull
step

\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{45}\mathrel{\rhd}\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{34}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}
.

2.

Second, recall that
\mu_{12}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}

and note that:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\mu_{234}&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{4}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{4}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{34}&\text{as $\mu_{34}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{4}$,
as shown above}\end{array}

Since
\mu_{2}
is non-empty,
this gives us a
\rhd
-Pull
step

\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{234}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}\mathrel{\rhd}\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{34}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}
.

With these two
\rhd
-Pull
steps, we are able to close the diagram:

\textstyle{\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{45}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\rhd
-Pull
\rhd
-Pull
\textstyle{\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{45}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\rhd
-Pull
\textstyle{\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{234}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\rhd
-Pull
\textstyle{\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{34}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{5}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\rho}

If
\mu
is a multistep, an
unfolding
of
\mu

is any sequential rewrite
\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}

such that
\mu\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}

and such that the
\mu_{i}
are not empty.
The remainder of this section
relies on the following assumption

on the HRS
\mathcal{R}
,
which allows to prove strong normalization and the standardization
theorem itself.

Assumption 1

(Finiteness condition)
.

For any given multistep
\mu
,
the length of the unfoldings
of
\mu
is bounded. We write
0pt{\mu}
for the length
of the longest unfolding of
\mu
,
called its depth.

Any strongly normalizing HRS enjoys the finiteness condition.
Unfortunately, the untyped
\lambda
-calculus does not.
For instance, given terms

\omega=\mathbf{lam}\,(\lambda x.\mathbf{app}\,x\,x)

and

\Omega=\mathbf{app}\,\omega\,\omega

and the rewrite

\rho=\beta\,(\lambda x.\mathbf{app}\,x\,x)\,\omega:\Omega\rightarrowtriangle\Omega
,
then

\beta\,(\lambda x.y)\,\Omega:\mathbf{app}\,(\mathbf{lam}\,(\lambda x.y))\,\Omega\rightarrowtriangle y

has arbitrarily long unfoldings:

\mathbf{app}\,(\mathbf{lam}\,(\lambda x.y))\,\rho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mathbf{app}\,(\mathbf{lam}\,(\lambda x.y))\,\rho\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\beta\,(\lambda x.y)\,\Omega
.

Proposition 59
.

Standardization is strongly normalizing,
and it is confluent up to
\mathrel{\asymp}
,
that is,
if
\rho\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\rho_{1}

and
\rho\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\rho_{2}

then there are
\rho^{\prime}_{1},\rho^{\prime}_{2}

such that
\rho_{1}\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\rho^{\prime}_{1}

and
\rho_{2}\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\rho^{\prime}_{2}

and
\rho^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\asymp}\rho^{\prime}_{2}
.

Proof 7.2
.

For the first item,
define the Sekar–Ramakrishnan measure
[
SR93
]

of a sequential rewrite
\rho

as a tuple of non-negative integers written
\mathbf{SR}(\rho)
defined by:

\mathbf{SR}(\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s})=(n,0pt{\mu_{n}},\ldots,0pt{\mu_{2}},0pt{\mu_{1}})

These tuples are partially ordered by declaring

(n,x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\succ(m,y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})
to hold
if either
n>m

or
n=m
and
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})>(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})
holds
according to the usual lexicographic order.
This order is well-founded.
To see that
\mathrel{\rhd}
is strongly normalizing, it suffices to note that
it decreases the Sekar–Ramakrishnan measure.
More precisely,

\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\sigma
implies
\mathbf{SR}(\rho)\succ\mathbf{SR}(\sigma)
.
Moreover,

\rho\mathrel{\asymp}\sigma
implies
\mathbf{SR}(\rho)=\mathbf{SR}(\sigma)
,
which means that
\mathrel{\rhd}
can be lifted to
\mathrel{\asymp}
-equivalence classes.
The second item is a consequence of Newman’s Lemma on
the rewriting relation
\mathrel{\rhd}
lifted to
\mathrel{\asymp}
-equivalence classes,
using local confluence (Lemma
58
).

Theorem 60

(Standardization)
.

Let
\rho,\sigma
be sequential rewrites.
Then

\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma

if and only if
there exist rewrites
\rho^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
in
\mathrel{\rhd}
-normal form
such that
\rho\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\rho^{\prime}

and
\sigma\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\sigma^{\prime}
and
\rho^{\prime}\mathrel{\asymp}\sigma^{\prime}
.

Proof 7.3
.

We prove that the following are equivalent:

1.

\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma

2.

\rho\mathrel{(\mathrel{\rhd}\cup\mathrel{\rhd}^{-1}\cup\mathrel{\asymp})^{*}}\sigma

3.

There exist rewrites
\rho^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}
in
\mathrel{\rhd}
-normal form
such that
\rho\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\rho^{\prime}

and
\sigma\mathrel{\rhd}^{*}\sigma^{\prime}

and
\rho^{\prime}\mathrel{\asymp}\sigma^{\prime}
.

•

(1\implies 2)

Since
\rho,\sigma
are sequential,
we may work implicitly up to the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Assoc
rule.
Doing this, it can be shown
that the equivalence
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma

must be derived from the reflexive, symmetric, and transitive closure
of following variant of the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm
rule:

\begin{array}[]{rllr}\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}&\mathrel{\sim}&\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}&\textsf{{$\mathrel{\sim}$-Perm'}}\\
&&&\text{if $\mu\Leftrightarrow\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}$}\end{array}

It suffices to show that if
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma

is derived using the
\mathrel{\sim}
-Perm’
axiom,
then
\rho\mathrel{(\mathrel{\rhd}\cup\mathrel{\rhd}^{-1}\cup\mathrel{\asymp})^{*}}\sigma
.
Indeed, let
\mu\Leftrightarrow\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}
.
We consider two cases, depending on whether
\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}

is empty or not:

1.

If
\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}
is empty.

Then
\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{2}^{\blacktriangleright}=\mu_{1}^{\blacktriangleleft}
.
Note that

\mu\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}

holds by Theorem
36
, using
\mathrel{\approx}
-IdR
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}&\mathrel{\asymp}&\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}\\
&\mathrel{\lhd}&\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}&\text{by {{$\rhd$-{Del}}}}\end{array}

2.

If
\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}
is non-empty.

On one hand,

\mu\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Moreover,

\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\blacktriangleright}

holds by Theorem
36
, using
\mathrel{\approx}
-IdR
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rlll}\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}&\mathrel{\lhd}&\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\blacktriangleright}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}&\text{by {{$\rhd$-{Del}}}}\\
&\mathrel{\lhd}&\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}&\text{by {{$\rhd$-{Pull}}}}\end{array}

•

(2\implies 3)

This is an immediate consequence of Proposition
59
.

•

(3\implies 1)

It suffices to show that
\mathrel{\rhd}\ \subseteq\ \mathrel{\sim}

and
\mathrel{\asymp}\ \subseteq\ \mathrel{\sim}
:

1.

\mathrel{\rhd}\ \subseteq\ \mathrel{\sim}
:
Let
\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\sigma
and
let us check that
\rho\mathrel{\sim}\sigma
.
We consider two subcases, depending on whether

\rho\mathrel{\rhd}\sigma
is derived from

\rhd
-Del
or
\rhd
-Pull
:

1.1

\rhd
-Del
:
The step is of the form:

\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}\mathrel{\rhd}\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}

where
\mu
is empty.
Then note that
\mu=\mu^{\blacktriangleright}=\xi_{i+1}^{\blacktriangleleft}
,
so by Theorem
36
, using
\mathrel{\approx}
-IdR
,
we have that:

\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}\mathrel{\sim}\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}

1.2

\rhd
-Pull
:
The step is of the form:

\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}\mathrel{\rhd}\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}

where
\mu_{12}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}

and
\mu_{23}\mathrel{\sim}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}

and
\mu_{2}
is non-empty.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&&\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{23}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{2}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}\\
&\mathrel{\sim}&\xi_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{12}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{3}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{i+1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}\\
\end{array}

2.

\mathrel{\asymp}\ \subseteq\ \mathrel{\sim}
:
Suppose that
\rho\mathrel{\asymp}\sigma
.
This means that

\rho=\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}

and
\sigma=\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}

where
\mu_{i}\mathrel{\sim}\nu_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then it is immediate to conclude that

\mu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}\mathrel{\sim}\nu_{1}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu_{n}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\underline{s}
.

8.
Related Work and Conclusions

As mentioned in the introduction, proof terms were introduced by van Oostrom and de Vrijer for first-order left-linear rewrite systems to study equivalence of reductions in
[
vOdV02
]
and
[
Ter03
, Chapter 9]
. They are inspired in Rewriting Logic
[
Mes92
]
. In the setting of HORs, Hilken
[
Hil96
]
introduces rewrites for
\beta\eta
-reduction together with a notion of permutation equivalence for those rewrites. He does not study permutation equivalence for arbitrary HORs nor formulate notions of projection. Hilken does, however, justify his equations through a categorical semantics. We have already discussed Bruggink’s work extensively
[
Bru08
,
Bru03
]
. Another attempt at devising proof terms for HOR by the authors of the present paper is
[
BB20
]
. The latter uses a term assignment for a minimal modal logic called Logic of Proofs (LP), to model rewrites. LP is a refinement of S4 in which the modality
\Box A
is refined to
[s]A
, where
s
is said to be a witness to the proof of
A
. The intuition is that terms and rewrites may be seen to belong to different stages of discourse; rewrites verse about terms. Terms are typed with simple types and rewrites are typed with a modal type
[s]A
where the term
s
is the source term of the rewrite. However, the notion of substitution that is required for subject reduction is arguably ad-hoc. In particular, substitution of a rewrite
\rho:s\rightarrowtriangle s^{\prime}:A
for
x
in another rewrite
\sigma:t\rightarrowtriangle t^{\prime}:A
is
defined
as the composed rewrite
\rho{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\bm{\{}}}x{\backslash}t{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\bm{\}}}}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}s^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\bm{\{}}}x{\backslash}\!\!{\backslash}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\bm{\}}}}
, where
\rho
is substituted for
x
in
\underline{t}
followed by
\sigma
where
s^{\prime}
is substituted for
x
.

Future work.
It would be of interest to develop tools based
on the work presented here for reasoning about computations
in higher-order rewriting, as has recently been explored for
first-order rewriting
[
KM18
,
KM19
]
.
One downside is that our
rewrites cannot be treated as terms in a higher-order rewrite system. Indeed,
rewrites are not defined modulo
\beta\eta
(for good reason
since an expression such as
(\lambda x.\rho)\,\sigma
should not be
subject to
\beta
reduction).

One problem that should be addressed is that of formulating
standardization
(see
e.g.

[
Ter03
, Section 8.5]
) using rewrites.
This amounts to giving a procedure that reorders the steps of a rewrite
\rho
,
yielding a rewrite
\rho^{*}
in which outermost steps are performed before
innermost ones.
Standardization finds canonical representatives of

\mathrel{\approx}
-equivalence classes, in the sense that

\rho\mathrel{\approx}\sigma
if and only if
\rho^{*}=\sigma^{*}
.
The flattening rewrite system of Section
5
is
a first approximation to standardization, since

\rho\mathrel{\approx}\sigma
if and only if

\rho^{\mathcal{F}}\mathrel{\sim}\sigma^{\mathcal{F}}
.
Section
7
presents a procedure
to compute canonical representatives of
\mathrel{\approx}
-equivalence classes,
based on the idea of repeatedly converting

\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu
into
\mu^{\prime}\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu^{\prime}

whenever
\nu\Leftrightarrow\xi\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\nu^{\prime}

and
\mu^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow\mu\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\xi
,
an idea reminiscent of
greedy decompositions

[
DDG+15
]
.
Unfortunately, this procedure does not always terminate,
due to the fact that rewrites may have infinitely long “unfoldings”;
for instance, if
\varrho:\mathbf{c}\rightarrowtriangle\mathbf{c}

and
\vartheta:\mathbf{f}(x)\rightarrowtriangle\mathbf{d}

then
\vartheta(\mathbf{c}):\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{c})\rightarrowtriangle\mathbf{d}

is equivalent to arbitrarily long rewrites of the form

\mathbf{f}(\varrho)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\ldots\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\mathbf{f}(\varrho)\mathrel{{\bm{;}}}\vartheta(\mathbf{c})
.
A terminating procedure should probably rely on a measure based on the
notion of
essential development

[
vO99
, Definition 11]
.

Another avenue to pursue is to characterize permutation equivalence via

labelling
. The application of a rewrite step leaves a witness in the term
itself, manifested as a decoration (a label). These labels thus collect and
record the history of a computation. By comparing them one can determine
whether two computations are equivalent. Labelling equivalence for first-order
rewriting is studied by van Oostrom and de Vrijer
in
[
vOdV02
]
and
[
Ter03
, Chapter 9]
.

We have given semantics to rewrites via Higher-Order Rewriting Logic. A categorical semantics for a similar notion of rewrite and permutation equivalence was presented by Hirshowitz
[
Hir13
]
(projection equivalence and flattening are not studied though). Our
s{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\bm{\{}}}x{\backslash}\!\!{\backslash}\rho{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{\bm{\}}}}
is called
left whiskering
and
\rho{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\bm{\{}}}x{\backslash}s{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\bm{\}}}}

right whiskering
, using the terminology of 2-category theory. These are then used to define
\rho{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\bm{\{}}}x{\backslash}\!\!{\backslash}\!\!{\backslash}\sigma{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\bm{\}}}}
. A precise relation between the two notions of rewrite should be investigated.
